ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u2	Thank you very much. We proceed with the examination of item 15 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the governmental draft law amending the law on the shaping of the agricultural system (prints No 1975 and 1982). I ask Mr Jan Krzysztof Ardanowski to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u3	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report from two committees: the Committee on Energy and the Treasury and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the amendment of the law on the shaping of the agricultural system. At the order of the Marshal, the committees at the joint meeting on 6 November considered draft printing law 1975. The interesting discussion initiated by the government's authorised representative for strategic energy infrastructure, Minister Piotr Naimski, concerned not so much the new powers for the three companies of the Treasury, in respect of which the law simplifies the purchasing procedures necessary for the expansion of both electric transmission networks, i.e. companies that carry out energy transmission in Poland... This thread generally did not cause controversy among Members. Rather, it was discussed about other solutions in the law on the shaping of the agricultural system. This is a dispute that has been going on since significant changes to this law. I would like to remind you that the bill entails a significant simplification of the acquisition of real estate or the right to perpetual use, as well as the acquisition of shares and shares in commercial companies, which are the owners of agricultural real estate, by three companies: PERN, Gaz-System and PSE - Polish Electro-energy Networks, in situations where the usual utility of transmission is not sufficient. This is most often the case for small agricultural areas where transformer stations, piston systems, pumps that are necessary for the transport of liquid fuels or gas or electricity have to be built. It seems that this solution - such voices were generally dominant in the discussion - is good because this law does not eliminate two special laws, so-called. special arrangements which, in particular situations, in the case of specific transmission investments, allow even compulsory land buy-out, if necessary. On the other hand, in the most common situation, when an investor agrees with a farmer, the owner of the land as to the price and subject of the transaction, the implementation of the current rules, which very strongly protect agricultural land and which make it necessary for any transaction concerning agricultural property to be notified to the National Agricultural Support Centre, a new institution which has taken over the rights and obligations of the Agricultural Property Agency, greatly extends the process of obtaining consent for such transactions. Besides, it would be absolutely strange if strategic companies of the State Treasury, which are responsible for the energy security of Poland, were blocked when it comes to the purchase of small agricultural parcels necessary for them, even by the right of first purchase from KOWR, the National Centre for Agricultural Support. That would be absolutely incomprehensible. Let me remind you that the Act on the shaping of the agricultural system specifically protects agricultural land, giving the state institution also the right to interfere in transactions. It seems that this law actually responds to the expectations of farmers, although it is not the Scriptures, where, as I mentioned at the committee meeting, neither text can be changed. It follows from the announcement of the Minister of Agriculture that this law will also be revised in various areas where its operation has shown some need for correction. But here we are not discussing the change of the entire law, but about this very detailed and supported by most Members who speak in the discussion, which facilitates strategic companies of the Treasury responsible for energy security of the acquisition of real estate, perpetual use rights or shares in commercial companies which are owners of agricultural property without undue delay, so that we can feel secure in terms of energy security through these investments. The committees, after examining this, ask the High Sejm to accept without amending the draft law on printing 1975. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u4	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Ewa Malik will be the first to speak on behalf of Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u5	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present an opinion on the draft law amending the law on the shaping of the agricultural system. The purpose of the proposed regulation is to facilitate commercial law companies whose sole shareholder or shareholder is the State Treasury for the transmission of electricity, gas fuels or liquid acquisition of rights to parcels on which the transmission infrastructure is to be established. This applies to only a few entities pursuing the objectives of ensuring energy security of the Republic of Poland, including Polish Electro-energy Networks SA, Gas-System SA and PERN SA. The draft law is intended to provide a solution in a situation where the burden on real estate becomes insufficient by the law of transmission service and in order to enable the investment to be carried out, it is necessary to acquire the aforementioned. real estate companies. At present, it is extremely difficult to acquire the aforementioned. agricultural real estate companies necessary to carry out the investment because of the need to conduct proceedings for the approval of the Director-General of the National Agricultural Support Centre to acquire the right to such real estate, and in some cases it is even extremely difficult because of the lack of the possibility of complying with the conditions for a positive decision. Also, the current provisions of the Act on the formation of the agricultural system make it difficult for companies belonging to capital groups to convert ownership, in which the State Treasury transfer companies are the parent companies. Then obtaining the consent of the Director of KOWR to transfer the right to real estate between companies belonging to the same capital group requires the disclosure of information regarding the possibility of acquiring real estate, which may lead to a breach of the energy security of the Republic of Poland as a result of the availability of knowledge regarding the management of real estate belonging to companies carrying out strategic activities and infrastructure on them. For all these reasons, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I recommend that the High Sejm adopt an amendment to the law on shaping the agricultural system contained in the Parliamentary Printing No 1975. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u6	Thank you very much. Mr Kazimierz Plocke will now speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u7	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! The government's draft amendment to the law on the shaping of the agricultural system is another attempt to patch this harmful law, which, I will remind you, protect the Polish land from redemption by foreigners, while mainly creates problems for Polish families, farmers, entrepreneurs, local governments and, as it turns out, also state companies. The Act has already been amended several times by ad hoc partial amendments, as it turned out that its rules interfere with the normal functioning of the economy. For example, in July 2006 the restriction on the value of mortgages on agricultural property had to be removed from the Act, because the banks ceased to accept the land as collateral for loans. That didn't help much, because the bail bonds are still subject to the bill. Only individual farmers and entities with the permission of the National Agricultural Support Centre to acquire these properties may join them. The problem is that it is virtually impossible to obtain such consent before the auction, and this is detrimental to the interests of creditors and debtors themselves, including farmers. Agricultural real estate is still not treated as reliable collateral, especially by banks. This situation was further aggravated by a regulation issued this year by the Minister of Justice excluding a large part of the agricultural holding's assets from bail-out. Under the rigour of the law, traffic was also excluded because it turned out that in the light of the Agricultural System Act they are treated as agricultural real estate. The acquisition of land by national parks was also excluded. We would like to point out that this year the Mining Act excluded from the action of the Act on the formation of the agricultural system the acquisition of agricultural land by mining companies related to mines. Why did you have to do that? Because the provisions of the Act on the shaping of the agricultural system have hindered the restructuring of the mining sector. Today we deal with state transport operators. There are certainly still many places in the economy where irrational provisions of the agricultural system law make it difficult for companies carrying out public tasks. We predict that in the near future there will be a queue of other resorts, companies or environments demanding further amendments to the agricultural system law. In the case of citizens - and this is very interesting - the said Act on the agricultural system makes it difficult, first, to purchase agricultural land, second, to build houses by Polish families, because wherever there is no spatial planning, land with an area of 3,000. m2 and larger are treated as agricultural land and the approval of the President of the National Agricultural Support Centre is required for their purchase. After they have been purchased for 10 years, the land must be grown in person, not sold or leased. In view of this situation, I would also like to ask the question: Was it the will of the legislator that investments in Poland should only concern agricultural production? The practice so far shows that the government is implementing the scheme "Housing+', and it is very noble actions, but we are afraid that as a result of the act on shaping the agricultural system we will have programmes: home- and investment-. The Civic Platform points out that the provisions of the Act on the formation of the agricultural system complicate the process of distribution of inheritance assets in families, property of spouses after divorce or other abolition of joint ownership. In the light of this law, the in-laws are not close to their child's spouse, and the nephew cannot dispose of agricultural property for his uncle. Very complicated, but the point is to simplify these rules. This shows that the law on the shaping of the agricultural system applies profoundly even to intra-family property relations. That makes it harder for the inheritance process. The provisions proposed by the government to amend the agricultural system law may be justified but may not be selective. We believe that we should deal with a comprehensive decision. Therefore, Mr. Marshal, the Civic Platform is proposing amendments to this project and our club's final position will be followed by an analysis of the amendments tabled by our club. Thank you. I would like to make it clear that the emergence of transmission networks is very important and I do not deny the need to create them. However, it should be noted that the basic institution serving this purpose is the serviceability of the transmission, not the buying and gathering of land. In my opinion, making it easier to purchase land for a number of operators will increase the number of cases where, instead of establishing transport service, it will simply buy land because it will simply be easier. Why negotiate with citizens the amount of the service charge if you can buy land or at least suggest it? The Director of KOWR, and by him the Minister of Agriculture, will have no impact on the acquisition of agricultural land. This is a very worrying phenomenon which may be a potential source of irregularities. In conclusion, the project may lead to undue concentration of agricultural land by companies. Farmers need arable land, not set aside in the hands of the state. Moreover, it is scandalous to deprive the Minister of Agriculture of control of the marketing of agricultural property, and I am surprised that the representatives of the Ministry of Agriculture voluntarily waive not only their right but their obligation. So who, if not the Director of KOWR or the Minister of Agriculture, will be responsible for the regularity of farming land management in Poland? I'm gonna point out another shocking fact. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u41	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present to the State a draft law on the State Protection Service, which will replace the previous service, this project will replace the Act of 16 March 2001 on the Government Protection Office. At the outset, a few words explain why I am submitting this draft, a government project to the High House. Well, when I became Minister of Interior and Administration 2 years ago, I found this service - the Office for Government Protection - in bad condition. Officers have always been a value in this service. Here I stress the professionalism, preparation, involvement of government protection officers in daily service and their self-sacrifice. This is the value that was, is and will be. On the other hand, when an audit of this service was carried out, it showed many years of negligence, even dramatic underfunding of the Office for Government Protection. The effects of these negligence have increased over the years. The answer of the Law and Justice Government was a programme to modernise uniformed services, which is also covered by the Office for Government Protection, and thus to allocate additional money to three levels: firstly, to modern equipment, secondly, to infrastructure and thirdly to increases for officers. Last year two important events took place in Poland, i.e. NATO and World Youth Days Summit. The Office of Government Protection was involved in the preparation and safe conduct of these events. Here, indeed, once again, I would like to emphasise the professionalism, commitment and dedication of the officers. These two events caused us to present government presentations only this year. We are responsible for what concerns security. Therefore, we are only presenting the project to you now and not last year. I was talking about this collapse, about the dramatic underfunding of the government security office. The symbol of all this negative that was happening at the Government Protection Office is the figure of his boss, today the policy of the Civic Platform, a man who did not suffer any consequences for negligence concerning the failure to prepare the visit of the President of the Republic of Poland. Professor Lech Kaczyński in Smolensk on 10 April 2010 More, the former head of the Office for Government Protection at the hands of former President Komorowski received a general promotion after this tragedy, after this dramatic event, which by the National Assembly was regarded as the greatest tragedy after World War II. Today we can hear that this former head of the BOR, the Civic Platform's politician, focuses his political commitment on restoring the privileges of the esbeks. That's the symbol of a man who's been at the head of the government's security office for many years. It's a symbol of all the bad things that happened at the government security office. High Izbo! As I said, the scale of negligence and omissions at the Government Protection Office, which was revealed by the audit carried out in December 2015, concerns both the inadequate preparation of the formation for contemporary threats and the dramatic underfinancing of the formation budget or the lack of a concept of modernising the existing organisational structure. All this justifies the need for a profound change in the legal situation in force in this regard. As a result, we considered it desirable to thoroughly modify the functioning of the service in terms of organisation and law, leading to the creation of a completely new quality protective formation. The establishment of a new formation will, firstly, allow for an increase in the standard of implementation of protection and prevention measures, secondly, a precise definition of the tasks of the formation and the adjustment of powers to them, thirdly, the creation of a new model of service conduct, fourthly, the use of new means to verify the suitability of candidates for service and the verification of officers to occupy certain posts, fourthly, the creation of a new incentive system. The creation of a new service will also contribute to the creation of staff free from assigning them negative assessments of the operation of the BOR in its current form and will allow for a fair assessment of the activities of the service. The Law on a new protective formation - the State Protection Service is a response to the needs of the practice of protective measures and, above all, will ensure the effectiveness and immediateness of the formation responsible for the protection of persons and facilities of key importance for the functioning of the State. It will also help to clarify and clarify the rules by clarifying a number of interpretation doubts raised during the almost 16-year period of application of the Government Protection Office Act. Changing threats to the safety of persons and facilities of key importance for the functioning of the State, including terrorist threats, require adaptation to them both of the protective tactics and methods of carrying out exploratory actions. This results in the need to modify the existing scope of powers and the way in which the protective formation operates, including the granting of its right to perform operational and exploratory activities in order to obtain information on the risks relating to protected persons and facilities, and the prevention of offences that pose a direct threat to the safety of those persons and facilities. Changes also require a way of organising a service, the new structure of which should be adapted to the current risks. Security of the protected requires the introduction of new rules for recruitment into the service, among others. by enabling the head of the new formation responsible for its proper functioning to verify applicants for admission to service by subjecting them to psychophysiological examinations. High Seym! With regard to the protection of persons and objects so far implemented, the service pragmatism of the new formation fundamentally corrects the tasks of the BOR and extends the catalogue of powers enabling them to be implemented. This will also strengthen the position of the service and its chief, the commander of the State Security Service. The new shape of the task was aimed at ensuring absolute security for a specific circle of people and objects serving them. A new protective formation will also be added a new task of identifying and preventing certain types of crime against protected persons. This task is part of the intended extension of competences to strengthen the comprehensive dimension of the security formation by granting a new protective formation the right to take operational and exploratory action. The new formation will be equipped with the competence to carry out operational and exploratory activities in forms defined in accordance with the tasks to obtain information on the risks relating to protected persons and facilities, as well as the prevention of criminal offences constituting a direct threat to the safety of those persons and facilities. In the current law governing the operation of the BOR, this service has been entitled to carry out preventive activities, although partly close to operational and research activities, but not sufficient from the perspective of the specifics of the current safety risks and the under-defined by law. The extension of the powers of the new formation will benefit the effectiveness of its activities. It will cease to be dependent solely on information obtained from other services and will gain some degree of independence. In the context of today's security threats, it is impossible to imagine fully effective measures to ensure the safety of persons and protected facilities without the possibility of performing operational and reconnaissance activities. This solution will not only increase the ability of the Service to obtain information that is essential for the security of protected persons and facilities on its own, but will, at the same time, extend the possibilities for obtaining information obtained through the conduct of operational and exploratory activities by other services for which the Government Protection Office has so far not been an equivalent partner due to limited competence. The primary area in which surgical and cognitive activities will be performed will be to ensure the safety of persons and protected facilities. Operational control was also allowed in the case of offences committed by officials and employees of the State Protection Service in connection with the performance of their duties. I would like to stress that State Protection Officers in the field of operational and exploratory activities will be subject to identical formal requirements and control mechanisms, such as representatives of other services with such powers, including judicial review. This solution therefore meets the Polish constitutional standard, the requirement to provide independent authorities with control and supervisory instruments. In relation to the previous tasks of the BOR, the range of facilities under the protection of the new formation will be extended. The protection will be subject not only to facilities serving the President of the Republic of Poland, the President of the Council of Ministers, the Minister responsible for Home Affairs and the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs, but also to certain seats of other members of the Council of Ministers indicated in the decision of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, excluding the premises of the Ministry of National Defence and the Ministry of Justice. This will create a system of security for government facilities guaranteeing the highest standard of protection, replacing often illusory services in this area of private entities. These solutions will require an increase in the status of permanent formation, as well as an improvement in the training of its officers. In view of the appropriateness of focusing the security formation's activities on the protection of the most important people in the country and the facilities they serve in the country, the project provides that, as a rule, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs will be responsible for the protection of foreign offices of the Republic of Poland located at the place of stationing of Polish military quotas and the protection of diplomatic and consulary staff of these institutions will be responsible for the protection of the military Zandarmery. The previous practice of the BOR has shown that it is too small a formation which does not have sufficient forces and means for the full effective protection of these facilities. However, bearing in mind that the protection of foreign offices by the Military Gendarmerie will be limited to the places of stationing Polish military quotas, it is proposed to introduce an additional form of protection of foreign offices applicable in special situations from the perspective of state security. The President of the Council of Ministers, in cases justified by security reasons, with respect to the protection of classified information, will be able to decide on the protection of selected foreign offices of the Republic of Poland by the State Protection Service if the protection of foreign offices is otherwise proven or may not be sufficient. The decision will be taken at the request of the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs after obtaining the opinion of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, taking into account the forces and means at the disposal of the State Protection Service, the risk assessment of these facilities and the possible scope and means of ensuring protection. Also in the case of the task of protecting objects and equipment of particular importance and ensuring their functioning, the practice has shown that the Government Protection Office is too small a formation without sufficient forces and means for the fully effective protection of these objects and devices. The implementation of this task by the Government Protection Office is particularly difficult also because it is not only limited to ensuring protection but also ensures its functioning, which in turn exceeds the financial and organisational capacity of the formations. This task was taken over by means of the solutions laid down in the Act of 16 March 2001 on the Office for Government Protection from the then liquidated Nadwiślanski Military Units. It is also impossible to use these facilities in connection with the implementation of the statutory activities of the formation, as their use must be linked to the field of defence. Thus, these objects cannot be used e.g. to evacuate protected persons in the event of an emergency. With this in mind, the draft law establishes the acquisition of this task by the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army. In addition, the draft Law on the State Protection Service defines a circle of protected persons who are part of a delegation of foreign countries residing in the territory of the Republic of Poland in relation to those listed in the Act on the Office for Government Protection. The principle of reciprocity was followed in this respect. The protection shall be covered by delegations consisting of persons with the status of head of state, the head of government and their alternates, the President of Parliament or the Chamber of Parliament or the Minister for Foreign Affairs. The procedure for determining the scope of protection of individuals protected by the new formation will also be improved. The decision to determine the scope of the protective measures to be taken against persons covered by the State Protection Service by law or on the basis of a separate decision of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs will be taken by the Head of the State Protection Service, informing the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. In the current legal situation, it is the Home Affairs Minister who decides on the scope of protection, while the Chief of the Office for Government Protection has the widest knowledge of potential threats and ways of countering them. The proposed amendment will improve the implementation of safeguard measures. Depending on the circumstances and needs, the Head of the State Protection Service will be able to immediately correct the scope of protection in the place of a significantly more extensive formal road with the participation of the Minister. On the other hand, the scope of the protection of the premises will be defined by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs after obtaining the opinion of the Head of State Protection Service, taking into account the risk assessment of these facilities. In the case of objects serving the President of the Republic of Poland and the President of the Council of Ministers, the Minister responsible for Home Affairs shall decide in agreement with the Head of the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland and the Head of the Office of the President of the Council of Ministers respectively. High Seym! The project also complements the long-term gap in the rules governing the functioning of the protective formation relating to the possibility of temporary withdrawal of protected persons from their protection, excluding measures to obtain information on direct threats to protected persons from whom the protective formation never ceases. It was also considered appropriate to grant the State Protection Service the right to benefit from other forms of assistance. In the event of a threat to the performance of security tasks, where the forces of the State Security Service prove insufficient or likely to prove insufficient, as well as in the event of a threat to the performance of security tasks in the territory of the Republic of Poland of foreign States delegations and other persons protected for the benefit of the State, it will be possible to provide assistance by soldiers of the Military Gendarmerie - on the basis of an order of the President of the Council of Ministers issued at the request of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, and police officers or Border Guard - on the basis of an order of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In order to ensure the safety of persons and protected facilities, it is necessary to build a strong position of the head of the service and of the formation itself, in addition to giving a new shape to the tasks of this service, which is directly linked to the improvement of the standard of protection. This will be done by providing the Chief of State Protection with the status of central government administration. Thus, it will be able to issue, on the basis of the authorisation provided for in the Act, regulations governing protection issues so far falling within the minister's competence, which will speed up and improve the decision-making process. Currently, similar categories of cases in other departments are governed by their bosses. At the same time, attention should be paid to the fact that all the heads of services that have been closely co-operating with the BOR so far are central government administrations and that the change will therefore contribute to a balance in this respect. In addition to the provision laying down the obligation of public entities to ensure the necessary conditions for the performance of their statutory tasks by the State Protection Service, the project introduces a standard imposing on protected persons and management or administrative bodies protected by the structures the obligation to promptly communicate to the State Protection Service any information affecting the safety of protected persons and facilities as well as to comply with the safety recommendations of protected persons and facilities issued by the State Protection Service. The practice of protective measures shows that the existence of such an obligation is necessary in order to illustrate the obligation which also rests on those covered by the protection and therefore the resulting powers of formation. In order to ensure the effectiveness of the protective measures taken, close cooperation between the operators indicated above is necessary. High Seym! The proposed law also provides for a change in the provisions in force in the BOR concerning salaries and allowances. In order to simplify the rules in place of several types of additives in the Government Protection Office, a single supplement will be introduced - so-called. Special allowance of at least 1% basic salary, but not more than 50% That paycheck. The amount of the special allowance will depend on the degree of difficulty, complexity and manner of carrying out the tasks performed by the officer, his professional qualifications, the effects of his work and the professional opinion preceding the determination of the special allowance. In addition, the amount of the special allowance of an officer who has obtained a service opinion with a general good or exemplary assessment shall not be less than 5%. basic salary. This solution will be flexible in order to adapt the amount of allowances to the type and mode of the tasks carried out on an ongoing basis, while ensuring a motivating dimension. Thus, an officer of the State Protection Service will be motivated, within the framework of the performance of his duties, to carry them out effectively and to translate them into a special allowance. It should be noted that the design of this solution is similar to that of the Central Anti-corruption Office officers. The provisions contained in the project also complement the gap in the rules on the transfer of officers between services, allowing for the acquisition into the service of new formations which are predisposed to its performance as police officers, Border Guard, State Fire Department, Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Intelligence Service, Military Counterintelligence Service, Prison Service, Customs and Treasury Service and Central Anti-corruption Office. The project also provides for provisions obliging all officers to make declarations of property. This will ensure the transparency of the formation and fits into the broad concept of anti-corruption prevention. The draft law also assumes that the heads of organisational cells of the State Protection Service will cooperate with the inspector of internal surveillance, whose appointment, as you know, is underway in the framework of a separate draft law. It also clarifies the rules relating to the disciplinary responsibility of the State Protection Service officers, in line with other services, with the aim of introducing appropriate rigors in terms of their tasks. I would like to stress that, in relation to officials of the Government Protection Office who will become State Protection Officers, it is intended to retain their existing powers, in particular housing rights. At the same time, the bill slightly modifies the solutions in the Act on the Office for the Protection of the Government concerning the powers and principles of the Marshal's Guard. High Seym! Increased tasks and powers require an extension of the permanent composition of the new protective formation compared to the current state at the Government Protection Office. By adopting the employment limit provided for by the 2017 Budget Act, the Government Protection Office has funds for the employment of more than 2200 officers and 300 employees. The tasks of protecting new premises which are the seat of the Council of Ministers, undertaking operational and exploratory activities or wider involvement in administrative proceedings determine an increase in the personal composition of the emerging formation by around 800 officers. Thus, the actual number of formation posts would reach around 3000 officers and 300 staff, which is planned by the end of 2022. It must be stressed that the scope of the task of protecting the premises of the members of the Council of Ministers would depend on the capacity to absorb new staff. Analyzing the potential of the Government Protection Office, i.e. the number of candidates applying for admission to service, the number of candidates who have passed all stages of the qualification procedure, organisational opportunities, including very important training, we assume that the new formation will be ready to complete this task after 5 years. This period is also determined by the need to adapt the facilities to be protected by the new formation. Given that the vast majority of them do not have the required standard of security, it will also be necessary to change the structure of the buildings. It is necessary to provide the on-call facilities and their equipment, to prepare the deposit of weapons, the social and sanitary facilities or to install protective equipment. Getting the right permits and making the right adjustments, adaptation will require not only time, but also, of course, financial expenditure. The increase in employment in the new protective formation will of course generate costs for the state budget, however, it is necessary to create a service that will be able to adequately ensure the protection of the most important people and facilities in the country. High Seym! Finally, I would like you to take into consideration the assessment of the solutions presented in the draft, but I would like you to take into account that this is the first comprehensive reform of the protective formation since 2001, which will guarantee practical tools for effective protection of the most important people and objects in the country, commensurate with current challenges and threats to their security. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The changes concern, among others: the existing scope of powers, as well as the way the State Protection Service acted, previously the BOR. The explanatory memorandum also indicates an audit carried out in 2015 at the Office for Government Protection, which showed a significant scale of negligence and omissions, including: underfunding of this formation, as well as lack of proper implementation of tasks in the field of protection of Polish diplomatic representations, consulates and other representations outside Poland. Pointing to the scale of these omissions, the designers say that the bill proposes a new protective formation to live, which would lead to an increase in the standard of protection measures in the above mentioned areas. It assumes strengthening the position of the service and its chief, providing it with the status of a central government authority, as the Minister said, adding new tasks related to the identification and prevention of various types of crime. These are the things that were also reported here as doubts about the use of various forms of information acquisition and surveillance. The BOR had to be based on information from other services. Here we have the right to undertake operations and research activities and their financing from the operational fund. In addition, there is an increase in the position of office compared to the current situation in the BOR and the verification of the officers. Here we have various forms of verification, it is indicated even verification using a polygraph, using the help of other formations, among others. Military police, police, Border Guard. Officers of the new service will be required to make declarations of property. State Protection Officers will have the right to issue instructions to those who pose a threat, e.g. the order to leave the place, stop the vehicle, remove the vehicle, etc., legit, carry out personal checks, including baggage, carry out checks on persons entering protected facilities, include persons presenting a direct risk of life or health in order to communicate them to the relevant services, request assistance from public authorities, observe and record, by technical means, the picture of events in public places, and, in the case of operational and diagnostic and administrative activities, record the sound associated with these events. I will not repeat the details here. Ladies and gentlemen! They're divided sentences here. In general, I am not particularly attached to the names of the services in a mental sense, I am indifferent to what they are called, it is important that they actually meet the conditions or tasks to which they are called. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u109	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It has been my honor to present the position of the Polish People's Party to the Commission's draft law on the Marshal's Guard. As parliamentarians in this House, we have met more than one term on a daily basis, and we continue to meet the guards of the Marshal's Guards on the premises of the Sejm. The status of the guards of the Marshal's Guard until today has not been properly regulated or, most importantly, legally sanctioned. It is worth noting that, over time, in terms of the functioning of the Marshal's Guard, its employees have always done their duties well and exemplaryly, they have often served parliamentarians with considerable help, and they have presented themselves with dignity in front of citizens on the premises of the Parliament. It is also noted that the guards of the Marshal's Guard are people employed for employment contracts, with all the consequences resulting from the provisions of the Labour Code Act. The geopolitical situation, increased care for the property of the State Treasury and the obligation to protect the premises of the Sejm and the Senate make it necessary for the guards of the Marshal's Guard to act as officers, not employees with the right to give them the status of a public officer. The proposed regulation will not only increase the prestige of this service, increase the powers of future officers, but also align their position with that of other public servants. With regard to the provisions implementing the Marshal's Guard Act, it should be fully agreed that it would not be possible or justified to operate the Marshal's Guard Act itself without amending the provisions already in force listed in Chapter 2. As an example, I will point out amendments to the Civil Procedure Code Act or the Retirement Provision Act for Professional Soldiers and their Families or, for example, the Social Security Act. The adoption of the proposal to join the new service of the Marshal's Guard will not be mandatory, but will depend on the wish to take a vow according to a specific rota in the Marshal's Guard Act. Taking all this into account, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of directing the project to further legislative work. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u153	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The purpose of social security as an institution of a broadly understood social policy is to guarantee the insured person the income lost as a result of a specific random event, also called social risk. This principle, characteristic of social security law, is reflected in the mechanisms for determining the amount of social security contributions, broadly understood. As a general rule, the contribution is based on the remuneration of the activities which constitute the title of social insurance. The same principle is laid down in the rules governing the calculation of social security benefits. The size of the insurance benefit is based on the remuneration generated by the activities giving rise to the social security obligation. This principle has so far been fully implemented only in sickness insurance, accident insurance, general health insurance, but also as regards the system of guaranteeing benefits from the Labour Fund, i.e. the unemployment risk protection system as well as in the insurance against the risk of insolvency of the employer - it can be said that the benefits from the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund were also financed by contributions collected from the total remuneration of persons covered by such a guarantee. This principle has so far not been applicable in the field of pension insurance and pension insurance. We have, we are dealing with in this area, except for the principle, except actually this principle. The reason for whether it is part of this exception or whether the substance of this exception is the mechanical automatic limitation of the maximum annual base of the pension and pension contributions, which has been in force in our social security system since 1 January 1999. The essence of the project, which I have the pleasure of presenting to the High House today, a government bill amending the Social Security Act, is the proposal to abolish the limitation of the maximum annual basis for the assessment of pension and pension contributions, i.e. the proposal, can be said to include this general principle, which applies in the social security system, including pensions. [[Normalization.]] It can be said that this project also envisages abolishing the reduction of the benefit base, because the other side of the same medal, the consequence of the limitation of the maximum annual basis for pension and pension contributions is also a mechanical, automatic reduction in the amount of pension and pension benefits charged by persons who achieve revenues of more than 30 times the average monthly salary per year, i.e. an amount of 250% the average monthly salary per month. According to our findings, our calculations show that the number of people affected by this, as I said, by mechanical automatic limitation of the maximum annual basis for pension and pension contributions, amounts to around 350 thousand. In relative numbers, it is a group of persons below 2% of the total of persons subject to pension and pension insurance. The consequence of the abolition of the limitation of the maximum annual basis for pension and pension contributions will be the increase, the increase in pension and pension benefits of persons who will in the future fulfil the conditions for acquiring the right to adequate pension and pension benefits. Of course, the draft law is accompanied by an impact assessment of the regulation, including in this impact assessment of the regulation we pay attention to the consequences of the proposed legal solutions, the consequences also for the public finance system. If this is the will of the High House, I will be happy to answer any questions about the consequences of this regulation in the discussion. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Dear Minister, The current rules state that the annual basis for the assessment of pension contributions in a given calendar year may not be higher than the amount corresponding to 30 times the estimated average remuneration in the national economy for the calendar year concerned. The proposed bill aims to eliminate this upper limit. This means that if a person's income exceeds that amount, it will continue to receive contributions to pension and pension insurance, which will translate into a lower net remuneration. This also means that employers will bear higher employment costs for such people. Does the government not see the risk that both employees and employers will be strongly motivated to avoid paying increased contributions? Such a solution will not encourage the employment of professionals in the context of employment relationships, especially in the environment of new technologies and high competences. The abolition of the upper limit on pension and pension contributions will reduce the social security deficit briefly, but in the future will significantly increase it, increasing the imbalances in income and expenditure of the Social Insurance Fund. If pensions are calculated on the basis of the existing provisions, taking into account the elimination of the pension base ratio of 250%, expenditure from the Social Insurance Fund will also increase. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u226	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to you a government bill amending the Trade Union Act and some other laws, print No 1933. The main objective of the proposed amendments is to extend the scope of the right to establish and join trade unions in order to comply with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 2 June 2015, the signature of Act K 1/13. Freedom to establish trade unions is guaranteed in Article. 12 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland and with the freedom of trade unions is one of the basic features of our country's system. This fundamental guarantee develops and concreteizes the article. 59 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which ensures freedom of association in trade unions, provides for fundamental rights of trade unions, and establishes the principle of limitation of the right to coalition. It should also be indicated that in accordance with Article. 2 Convention No 87 of the International Labour Organisation on Freedom of Association and Protection of Trade Union Rights, adopted in San Francisco on 9 July 1948, workers and employers without any distinction shall have the right, without prior authorisation, to create organisations at their discretion and to join them, subject to compliance with their statutes. This Convention has been ratified by Poland and therefore in accordance with Article. 87. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland is a source of universal law in Poland. This justifies the need to amend the Polish Trade Union Act in order to implement both the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 2 June 2015 and to adapt its provisions to international standards. The proposed amendments aim at a full union coalition, i.e. the right to set up and join trade union organisations, all those who work for remuneration, regardless of the basis of employment, which may fall within the category of workers on constitutional and international grounds. The amendment of the law aims to enable the creation and entry of trade unions, in particular those carrying out work on the basis of civil law contracts, such as contract of order, contract of service or contract of works, and persons carrying out single-person business activities, carrying out work for the entity under contract. The work centre has consulted the project twice, in particular with representative trade union organisations and employers' organisations. During the drafting of the bill, the proposed solutions were also the subject of work in the framework of the Social Dialogue Council on Labour Law, as well as many meetings and discussions on the subject. However, the social partners failed to reach consensus on the proposed changes and as a result the decision on their final shape was on the government's side. However, we have been looking for solutions that would take into account the voice of the social partners, while at the same time providing workers with the opportunity to protect their rights and interests. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! With regard to the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 2 June 2015, which constitutes the basis for the amendments, it should be borne in mind that the Constitutional Court, in its explanatory memorandum, indicated that freedom of association in the trade unions referred to in Article. 59 (1) (a) 1 of the Constitution shall be entitled to any employee within the constitutional meaning. In the current legal state, however, the right to establish and join trade unions is not subject to such a wide range. It serves mainly within the meaning of the provisions of the Labour Code. The draft law therefore provides that the right to establish and organise trade unions will benefit those persons who can be regarded as workers within the constitutional meaning. These people have been named people who do work for a living. The person doing the work under the bill is both an employee within the meaning of the Labour Code and a person providing the work for remuneration on a different basis than the employment relationship if it fulfils the following conditions: firstly, he does not employ other persons for such work, regardless of the basis of employment, secondly, he does not bear the economic risk associated with the performance of the work, thirdly, he has such professional interests associated with the performance of the work, which may be collectively protected. I'll give you a brief overview of what each of the above conditions means. As regards the condition of non-employment for this type of work of other persons, it indicates the need to perform a particular type of work personally, i.e. without mediation of other persons, irrespective of what legal relationship such persons would have done their job. As far as the economic risk of activity is concerned, it exists e.g. where the person makes an investment, e.g. for the purchase or rental of appropriate equipment used for the execution of business orders and the materials necessary for the performance of business tasks. If the burden of paying the accounts, the payment of salaries to the auxiliary staff is on a person other than the worker, then there will be grounds to believe that such person bears the economic risk. The existence of contractual penalties for delay or delay in the performance of services or other elements of uncertainty as to the acquisition of income by a person other than a worker engaged in a job may also indicate an economic risk. Thirdly, the existence of such professional interests relating to the performance of work, which may be collectively protected. This condition was distinguished by the Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 2 June 2015. Professional interests, which can be protected collectively, are the interests for which trade unions are appointed and not the interests of individual persons engaged in employment. This will concern in particular the interests relating to the development of economic conditions for the performance of work, e.g. payment by employers of remuneration, but also other conditions for its execution, including social benefits, working conditions, issues related to the provision of appropriate facilities to the union organisation. The draft amendment also grants the right to join existing trade unions to volunteers, trainees and other persons who personally provide work without remuneration. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Among the other most important changes to the Act, which are essentially a consequence of extending the right of the union coalition to new entities, include: the introduction of protection mechanisms in the event of unequal treatment due to union membership; the possibility of taking into account, other than employees of workers engaged in employment in the number of persons who are required to obtain the rights of the union organisation, if these persons provide work for at least 6 months to the employer covered by the union organisation; the possibility of including a person belonging to several trade union organisations with the employer concerned only as a member of one of its designated trade union organisations; the introduction of a procedure to verify the number of works of the union organisation; currently, no instruments are available for verifying the figures provided to him by the trade union organisation; with a view to increasing the size of the union organisation due to the extension of the coalition law, it is also proposed to increase the threshold of representativeness for the trade union organisations; these thresholds have been increased from 7% to 8% for organisations forming part of the Social Dialogue Council and their member organisations and of 10% up to 15% for other organisations. The proposed thresholds will strengthen the most representative trade union organisations, which have a stronger mandate and will fully represent the rights and interests of workers. In crisis situations, representative organisations have a decisive vote when making final decisions. Trade union representation means the ability of trade union organisations to act as a representative of workers when more than one trade union organisation seeks to exercise that right. Following the extension of coalition law, it was assumed that new groups of entities entitled to join trade unions should be taken into account when determining the representativeness of the trade union organisation to which they are members. The other most important amendments introduced by the draft law include: granting to persons engaged in employment the right to exemption from work for the time necessary for the performance of ad hoc activities arising from their trade union functions outside the work establishment, if this activity cannot be carried out during their free time; including other than trade union workers, special protection against termination or termination of the contract; granting the possibility of an investigation by a person other than the worker performing the work of 6 months compensation for termination or termination by the employer without obtaining the agreement of the management board of the trade union organisation - however, due to the civil character of the contract for the work, contracts and orders of non-employees, such entitlement has not yet been granted; introducing deadlines for the presentation of the position of the trade union organisation on termination of the contract to a trade union activist 14 days and 7 days in the event of termination of the contract - the existing trade union law did not specify a time limit - in the absence of a trade union organisation's position within a time limit, it is assumed that the trade union organisation has given its consent to the termination or termination of employment with the protected person. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The next objective of the proposed amendment to the Trade Union Act is to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 13 May 2008. In the opinion of the Constitutional Court, the contested Article. Article 35 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 2nd Trade Union Act infringes the principle of the definition of criminal law. In view of the guidelines of the Constitutional Tribunal, the proposed regulation was added in Article. 35 of the Trade Unions Act. 2, which shall annul the conduct of persons performing trade union functions by: firstly, not informing the competent court of the changes to the statutes, secondly, by allocating the income from the trade union's activities for purposes not serving the performance of the statutory tasks of the union or sharing it between the members of the union, thirdly, by giving a higher number of members of the trade union organisation than that resulting from the facts in order to obtain the powers of the relevant trade union organisation. In such a case, a fine is imposed for the execution of the above-mentioned offences as a criminal sanction. We also make changes to other laws which result directly from the amendment of the Trade Union Act. We also introduce a 6-month period of vacathio legis, so that both employers and trade unions have the time to adapt to the changes being made. At the same time, by way of exception, the date of entry into force of the proposed Article has been postponed. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 6 Trade Unions Act 12 months from the date of the announcement. The proposed provision provides that when determining the number of persons engaged in employment, from which the 8- and 15-percent threshold for the representativeness of a trade union organisation is calculated, account shall be taken only of those persons engaged in employment which belonged to that organisation for a period of at least 6 months from accession to negotiations or arrangements. As of the date of entry into force of the Act, the scope of the right to create and join trade unions will be extended. There will also be increased representative thresholds for the trade union organisation. In the absence of a reservation as to the extended period of vacathio legis, the provision of Article. 25 In determining the representativeness of the trade union organisation in the period immediately following the date of entry into force of the Act, persons who were not entitled to a trade union coalition until the date of entry into force could not be taken into account and therefore not subject to the condition of at least 6 months of membership of the trade union organisation concerned. This would imply an unjustified differentiation of the legal situation of trade unions. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! A broad explanation of the changes can be found in the explanatory memorandum of the draft regulation. I ask the High House to proceed with the proposed amendment of the Trade Union Act and some other laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u238	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Trade Union Act and certain other laws. The amendment is due to the need to comply with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 2 June 2015. The main objective of the proposed amendments is to extend the law of the union coalition to persons engaged in work for work not covered by the provisions of Article. 2 (2) 1 Act on Trade Unions, e.g. persons engaged in work on the basis of civil law contracts or self-employed persons. So far, there were no consequences for the failure of the union organisation to provide information on the number of members. According to this project, the failure to provide information on the number of members of the organisation within the required time limit will result in the suspension of the trade union organisation's rights until the obligation has been fulfilled. In my view, this is the most rational solution that will affect the fair and timely provision of information on the number of members by union organisations. This is of great importance because the size of the union organisation translates into the possibility of exercising a number of rights and determines the extent of its powers. With the extension of the scope of the subject matter, the legal relationship of non-employees will become more sustainable. By introducing this regulation, the employer will not be able to freely terminate, terminate or amend the terms of a civil law agreement concluded with a trade union activist. In order to do that, he will have to get permission from the union board. This regulation leads to equalisation of the rights of employees in employment contracts and civil law contracts. The aim of the bill is also to amend the provisions clarifying and ordering the law, as well as the Labour Code and many other labour law laws. Ordering rules is simplifying them, which is what we all expect. I have no doubt that, with the entry into force of the Act, more Poles will be able to benefit from the protection offered by membership of trade unions. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceedings of the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u262	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a draft amendment to the Criminal Code and to the Code of Offences which are necessary as a result of a ruling by the Constitutional Tribunal which stated that the judgment of the offence of objects which were used to commit the offence, unless they were the property of the perpetrator, constitutes a violation of the Constitution, where there is a situation that the owner or other entitled person did not foresee that they could serve or be destined to commit the offence. The proposals therefore aim to introduce in the Article. 44 Penal Code § 7a, which states that if the objects mentioned in paragraph 2 are those which are forfeited, as I mentioned earlier, do not constitute the property of the perpetrator, their loss may be determined if their owner or other entitled person had predicted that they could serve or be destined to commit the offence, or could have foreseen this under the precautions required in the circumstances in question. The same solution is proposed in the Code of Offences, which is proposed in the article. 30 add-on § 2a, the wording of which would be: If the tools or other objects which served or were intended to commit the offence are not the property of the perpetrator, their loss may be determined if their owner or other entitled person had predicted that they could serve or be intended to commit the offence, or could have foreseen this under the precautions required under the circumstances in question. The Act shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. And that would be so much about the Senate's proposals. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u266	Mr. Marshal! Mr. Senator! High Izbo! As my previous speakers have said, this project is not controversial, adapting the Polish system of law to what the Constitutional Tribunal has ruled, and the Constitutional Tribunal has ruled that, in the event of the confiscation of an object which has been used for a crime, according to the legal condition that we have today, whether the owner of such a thing was aware of whether it was entrusted to someone or stolen, he may be subject to confiscation. We introduce into the Penal Code and the Enforcement Code a provision on the basis of which some kind of restriction is introduced, i.e. that this confiscation can only be decided if the person who owns the object that was used to commit the crime was aware that this object would be used in this way, or when he could assess, by accepting such normal conditions, a certain normal, ordinary awareness that such an object could be used for this purpose. Since it is, as I say, a non-controversial project, Kukiz will support it, because in fact it improves the situation of citizens who today can unfortunately be harmed by judicial judgments in a situation where some object, which is their property, can only be confiscated because someone used this thing to commit a crime. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u268	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I present the position of the club regarding the Senate draft law amending the Penal Code Act and the Code of Offences Act. The draft submitted aims to bring the system of law into line with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 October 2015, i.e. Act SK 59/13, in which the Court found that the judgment was incompatible with the Constitution of the Article. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 1 law on the prevention of drug addiction. According to that provision, in the event of conviction for offences related to the production, transport or placing on the market of narcotic drugs or psychotropic substances, and of granting or facilitating their use to others, the court may have ruled that the subject matter of the offence and the objects and tools which served or were intended to commit it were not the property of the perpetrator. In previous case-law, the courts did not refer at all to whether the owner of the objects or tools could have known or assumed that they would be used to commit the crime, and were ruled exclusively... Thus, the owner of the object or tools could also be deprived of ownership, abstracting entirely from his guilt as to the extent to which the offence was committed. The Polish Constitution, as well as the generally accepted principles of law, do not allow discretion when imposing criminal penalties. The decision should be subject to proof of guilt, to the commission of a prohibited act. It is therefore unacceptable to use repression for acts committed by another person, in particular with regard to a person who did not know and could not foresee with due care that the objects used to commit the offence may be used for that purpose. This is the state of affairs that the Senate's bill is repairing. It is only surprising that here the Senate serves the Minister of Justice, because we have, in principle, at every meeting of the Sejm, laws that accidentally change these acts - both the Penal Code and the Code of Offences. I think that several dozen projects of different kinds have been submitted. However, as we can see when the Polish legal system needs to be adapted to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, the Ministry of Justice remains passive. However, given that we are dealing with a project that improves the situation with regard to citizens' rights, as well as leads to the compatibility of the legal system with the Constitution, the Posel Club Modern requests that this project be referred to the committees for further work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

They appeared in a situation where the Audi car was converted to have a special locker to carry drugs, and the perpetrator of the crime was transporting drugs. The court ruled that this vehicle belonged to a third party, of course, and then the owner appealed to the Constitutional Court with a constitutional complaint, indicating that it was incompatible with the constitution that he, as a third party, had in no way committed a criminal offence, without having any charges - I stress - the criminal lost his car. And from this situation we must go out to remember how this sentence must be executed. Because the sound proposed here is dangerous. It's insanely dangerous. It leads to the situation that you have said. I know my friend's dealing drugs, everyone around here knows that, but I'm lending him a car. Then there is the question: How is the court supposed to prove that I knew or could find out that this car is being used for drug trafficking? How? He's gonna ask me if you knew that this car... Of course I'll say no. How will the court deal with the burden of proof in this situation? After all, the same owner who lent the car was aware that this person was dealing drugs, and he knew it. And here we turn the cat upside down. We stand on the side of criminals in the name of civil rights protection. Civil rights are never used to protect criminals. Therefore, the wording of this provision is very dangerous. Of course, we can work on this further in committee, but, I stress, we need to introduce here a specific presumption that this owner knew about it or could easily find out that - of course, the counter-proof needs to be predicted here a special shift of the burden of proof - the person whose car was lent to whom he himself lent the car - after all, it was not the dwarfs who lent this car, only the owner, so he knew or could have found out - he was dealing drugs and that the car specifically converted, with a safe deposit box, would be used as a tool to commit a crime, as an instrument for committing a crime. And it's only on this owner that the opposite will rest, the burden of proof to prove that it isn't. After all, judgment is not the Holy Spirit. The prosecutor is not the Holy Ghost. How are they gonna lead this evidence? We're on the side, ladies and gentlemen, very bad, on the side of criminals, and it's drug criminals. Drug crime, which must be combated by all means and means, at this point in time does not deserve protection in any way. What is more - one more thing I would like to point out - the sound of this provision suggests that the owner of this car should be charged. 'Cause who's the person who knew he was using his second car to sell drugs? He's a sidekick, of course. In the meantime, the Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 28 October 2015 explicitly stated: It should be noted that repressive sanctions can be applied to an individual not only in connection with the offence, p. 21. The Court further points out: The differentiation of human behaviour may lead to a situation in which a person's behaviour will not, in fact, exhaust the signs of crime, but that behaviour may be negatively assessed from the point of view of law. And what's written here determines otherwise, you might say, makes us dependent on prior charges against this particular owner. The owner must be accused of helping him, and then I will be able to continue this procedure. This isn't right. This owner may be a completely third, independent person, in no way involved in criminal activity. That's what the Constitutional Court said. Therefore, this law still requires much, much more work so that we do not fire a child with a bath and do not make certain features on this wall of the fight against crime, and this drug crime, which is often organised crime, very dangerous, in the name of the defence of civil rights. Civil rights defense, yes, but not where it's about defending criminal rights. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u283	Thank you, Minister. Does the senator want to speak? [[Yes.]] You're welcome, Senator.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u284	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I wanted to answer the question asked by one of the Members, namely whether this regulation should be extended to other provisions which also provide for the forfeiture of the subject matter for committing the crime. Of course, this can be considered, because there are many such regulations, with the fact that we, as Senate, are carrying out judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal, and therefore, because the ruling concerned a particular case, we have limited ourselves to this issue only. On the other hand, as far as I can remember, similar regulations providing for the failure of an object to commit a crime are provided for in at least a dozen or so other legal regulations when introducing such a criminal part into the regulation of certain spheres of social life. But what I'm trying to say is... [[But it was about the anti-drug bill.]] Yeah, it's especially the anti-drug law, but there's a whole bunch of other laws. If we reached, for example, the laws on nature conservation or fisheries, etc., it would turn out that sometimes there are almost humorous provisions in respect of compliance with the provisions of the laws in question, through the creation of criminal rules, where criminal sanctions for certain behaviours are provided for. Unfortunately, if we were to address this issue, this would be beyond the powers of the Senate, which has undertaken to bring the legal situation into line with the specific ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal. And let me remind you that this is exactly what happened in the context of the anti-drug bill. Now, speaking of how we propose these changes, I would like to say that we are actually not making anything innovative here. Assuming that there is no such thing as responsibility for objective occurrence of a particular event in Polish criminal law, only responsibility is always linked to the sphere of a certain probabilities of proceedings in the form of whether it is actions or omissions, we have taken this in a manner similar to that which occurs e.g. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 31a Tax Code Act. If you read these wordings, you will see that they are the same as the ones we have adopted here, assuming that in this way we are fulfilling this request, which is the result of the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal, and at the same time this is a more precise one, which, as I say, is based on the wording already in the Tax Code Act and therefore have a specific connotation, a specific caselaw, an interpretation widely accepted by various judicial instances. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u485	Thank you very much, thank you. The Secretary of State in the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers, a representative of the government for strategic energy infrastructure Piotr Naimski, asks for the floor. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u486	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all... [[Start with the other one.]] ...a few words about this bill. The Act is an amendment that facilitates the acquisition of agricultural land by three actually defined entities, which are not ordinary companies of the State Treasury, but are strategic entities, tools of the Polish state. These are transmission system operators - energy, gas and fuel. As important entities for State security, they must also carry out investment activities - they must carry out investment activities which often require the purchase of agricultural land. The liberalisation, and indeed the facilitation, of the removal from the regime which is currently in force under the law, of the purchase process by these land companies is an facilitation, which is primarily intended to provide a security where agricultural parcels which are required to expand the activities of operators can move into other hands. Under no circumstances does this law override the obligation to sell land to operators. It is only a law that allows this transaction to be carried out in a completely market manner, but without proving in the public process that no one other than the operator is showing interest in this land, because this is one of the conditions that allows the Director General of the national agricultural centre to grant consent to the transaction. This law, therefore, does not restrict farmers' rights to land or the right to acquire or dispose of agricultural land in any way. I will tell you more, she makes it easier for those farmers who want to sell their land, to sell it not only to other farmers, but also to those operators. The price of this transaction will, of course, be market-oriented and, as I say, there is no obligation on the landowner to sell it. On the other hand, I would like to say two words on a subject which is not in my competence, which falls within the competence of the Minister for Agriculture, and concerns a broader discussion here on the law on the shaping of the agricultural system. Well, the law in force proved to be a very good law, because the pathologies that took place in the land were stopped by that law. In the course of its implementation and at the same time the conduct of various economic processes has emerged from the experience of requests which lead to the amendment of this law, to allow for easier trading in situations that actually require it. Today's amendment is one such situation. By doing so, we will have at all times under the control of the fundamental purpose of this law, namely the sealing of agricultural land. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Unfortunately, however, it still seems that the situation of women remains more difficult than that of men.
