On 19 March 2009, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 The High Chamber adopted an amendment to the Law on the Education System, which not only consisted in subjecting 6-year-old children to school duties, but also gave full freedom to local authorities in the field of school closures, setting up school circuits and granting permits to establish public schools run by other entities than local self-government units. It also allowed the self-government to get rid of all schools in extreme cases. This was possible by transferring schools to foundations, associations or other entities. As a result of this amendment, the Polish education system has been experiencing undesirable changes which could not be stopped by the school curator or by the Minister of National Education under the regulations adopted. Some units of local government have eliminated their schools in advance, assuming that they will be replaced by non-governmental schools. Such actions resulted in reductions in education expenditure for local government units, mainly due to changes in the way teachers are employed. This phenomenon was highlighted by the Supreme Audit Chamber in discussing the results of the audit. The impact of the liquidation of public schools on the conditions for carrying out the educational tasks of municipalities. The Supreme Audit Chamber has identified concrete examples here. The results presented indicated two phenomena: a significant increase in teachers' salaries, i.e. the number of hours, and a significant reduction in teachers' salaries. Consequently, this resulted in the so-called. savings for local governments. At this point, it should be stressed, as the NIK also pointed out, that part of the school's liquidation was due to the progressive demographic low, which is obviously a fact. From 2009 to 2016, the number of children and young people attending local primary schools, junior high schools, high schools, technicians, primary vocational schools decreased by more than 12%. In the Mazowieckie voivodship, the decrease in the number of pupils was only 2.26%, which shows how this really applies to the issue of the low-level and liquidation. With regard to the data available to the Minister for National Education, there are no detailed data on the liquidation of schools in 2009, 2010 and 2011, because the educational information system only indicates numerical examples of changes in schools, i.e. this cannot be specified in detail. On the other hand, data has been collected by education curators and Minister of National Education since 2012. By comparing these data and data obtained from the educational information system, we can say that within 4 years - from 2011 to 2015 - local government units eliminated 1327 primary schools, junior high schools, general education schools, technicians and basic vocational schools for children and young people throughout the country. This statistics cover only those schools for young people who were self-government, whose liquidation was not due to a change in the school system, i.e. the liquidation of a particular type of school, such as. Profiled high school. Among those 1327 schools, 1085 schools showed the number of pupils, in other words, it was about 60,000. students. If we were to compare the data on teachers, taking into account the fact that based on the educational information system, we could identify only self-employed schools not functioning in teams, then we could say that 7,000 were employed in schools that were wound up. teachers, and the jobs were 5413. With regard to the Mazowieckie voivodship itself, 147 schools for children and young people, including 106 primary schools, 9 secondary schools, 13 general schools, 12 technicians and 7 basic vocational schools, have been removed during the four years we can use this data. It is also worth looking at that in the case of Mazowsze it represents as much as 7% schools over the years. More than 3,000 attended them. students. In other words, most of these schools were students. As regards the analysis of this system, which was adopted on 29 December 2005, the law changed the educational system, including strengthening the role of the school's curator, i.e. the school's liquidation requires a positive opinion of the curator, and therefore the state is now more effectively controlling the issue of the liquidation of schools, in particular the small schools which, thanks to these statutory changes, were able to save themselves and, together with the education reform, these schools could turn into 8-year primary schools, because the problem that you spoke about was mainly the primary schools. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u724	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Station investments were in operation before 2015. Between 2007 and 2015, investments were carried out for 121 plants in Poland, while there was no such station investment programme as was adopted by the current Ministry, by PKP SA and of course approved by the owner, which is represented by the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction. Previous investments were carried out in a rather chaotic way, because from year to year some stations were selected and investments were carried out. Sometimes investments were made concerning the building, sometimes platforms or tracks themselves, sometimes both could be realised. The station's investment programme, which is adopted, provides for a comprehensive implementation by 2023, i.e. if something is already implemented, it will always be implemented comprehensively, both the station building and the platforms including the railway line. They will certainly be implemented where new investments are planned in the form of modernisation of the railway line. At the current 189 stations we plan to spend about 1.4 billion. It's going to be a period of about six years. Previously, over 8 years, investments were made for 121 stations in the amount of approx. PLN 1 billion. It was, I repeat, done without the program and in a rather chaotic way. At the moment there is a programme - this is the answer to further questions - which provides for specific criteria adopted for the implementation of this programme. First of all, we want to increase access for passengers, we want to categorize the stations and modernise them, we want to cooperate with local government units, locate integrated connecting nodes, take into account current investment and renovation activities of the PKP, the degree of progress of the work on previously prepared investments or investments already carried out, or already carried out investment work, and the exclusion from modernisation of stations completed after 2010, i.e. those carried out before 2010. The categories of railway lines at which these stations are located are also introduced. It's a priority. A, i.e. high - stations located on railway lines modernised under the program. Priority B, i.e. mid-stations located on modernised lines, which are in the process of upgrading or planned to be upgraded in the perspective of 2023. Priority C, i.e. low-stations located on non-modernised lines and not included in the modernisation plans in the perspective of 2023. We take into account the social criterion, including the aspects of the country's sustainable economic and social development, the criterion of investment activities started and the criterion of measurable passenger flows, taking into account the daily number of train stops, based on data from the Railway Transport Office. As a result of the above process, the ranking of the project groups covered by the station's investment programme was created. We also define the need for modernization of stations to establish and close a list of projects. This allowed PKP to prepare investment plans, which require cooperation between PKP, which owns the stations, and PKP Polskie Linie Kolejowe, which owns railway lines and platforms. Unfortunately, because there was no integrated programme, so far there has been no cooperation and there have been such absurd situations that the building has been renovated, not renovated platforms, or vice versa - platforms have been renovated, upgraded and not modernised. At the moment, it is clear that the needs of people with disabilities or women with children in wheelchairs are taken into account in any modernization. Of course, this is the standard for any modernization at the moment. The needs of residents are also taken into account, often outside the scope of travel services, as well as the needs of local authorities by making part of the area available. This is generally the case for individual contacts between the local government and one or the other company, depending on what this cooperation is about. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 1a The Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund is the lowest pension, which amounts to PLN 1000. As you have rightly pointed out, this is 750 PLN. Therefore, this PLN 750 constitutes the amount of the guarantee from which it cannot be waived. Therefore, it is not possible to make the kind of interpretation that the bailiffs make. And here is the full agreement with the president of ZUS Mrs Uścińska, as well as fully agree with the position of Deputy Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy of Mrs Elżbieta Bojanowska, who in a letter dated 3 November this year. indicated that the interpretation of bailiffs is incompatible with the provisions of Article. 139 and Article 139 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 141 of the indicated law on pensions. In view of the above, the changes we propose, the solutions we propose, are primarily supervisory solutions concerning the supervision of bailiffs. All these cases are and will be carefully investigated by the Ministry in terms of possible disciplinary proceedings against bailiffs. It is then about checking all enforcement files as to whether or not there has been a serious infringement of the law in these cases, which could result in this type of liability. We have already asked the President of the District Court in Warsaw and the President of the District Court of Warsaw-Prague to determine on the basis of the enforcement file what the facts were and whether such an infringement occurred. The answers obtained will be analysed by us and, depending on the outcome, a decision will be taken on the follow-up of supervisory activities. This is the first case. The second point concerns organisational changes, of which the President also informed in her letter. Indeed, it is necessary to consider this, whether it is appropriate to introduce appropriate legislative changes, so that there is no such doubt, because, remember, in this case, it is the lowest pension of PLN 750, which is the minimum existence, the minimum social one that cannot be deducted from for the second time. In fact, the interpretation of the bailiffs would lead to a double deduction, as the honourable Member rightly pointed out, of the amounts for both health insurance contributions and advances and other claims on personal income tax. We'd just count it down twice. Hence, this interpretation is difficult to agree with. This is why legislative changes on this issue actually seem justified. The last point concerns legislative changes concerning the imposition of fines on the staff of the organisational units mentioned by the President in the letter. Indeed, it can be considered whether such conduct is appropriate in this case and whether any changes would be advisable. These are the actions we're already taking. Depending on the query made on the information provided by the courts, we will take further action. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u763	Thank you very much. I'm asking the Minister for an answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u764	As I mentioned in the Pact, it has a design procedure and those projects that are being implemented are implemented in the short and long term. An example is the food platform or intermodal freight terminal, which is designed e.g. in the port of Gdansk. This is a certain perspective of specific institutions. As far as the longer term is concerned, it is primarily about the objectives of the common agricultural policy, i.e. climate-related. This is the whole work procedure for farmers not to be affected by what awaits us after 2020. It's about new technologies, it's about transferring knowledge and information, it's about what's going to protect our environment. It's also about nitrate emissions. Long-term action concerns the taking of all work related not only to infrastructure that cannot be built in one or two years, but also to the outlook for climate change, to the UN 2030 Agenda and to those prospects that arise in connection with the new programming period. There are many of these elements, but it is about sustainable development, where, on the one hand, economic-oriented activities taking into account the social dimension and, on the other hand, environmental protection must be linked. These objectives are also made by the Ministry of Agriculture and the commitment is forward-looking - that is, on the part of the Ministry - that farmers should not cover costs, should not be burdened with this new policy related to new technologies, with carbon emissions, with ammonia emissions, with new investments, with methane emissions, with the reduction of production, so that these costs are as low as possible. This is also aimed at the vitality of rural areas, the quality of life, the building of a community - the local community and the search for directional solutions that will enable rural life to be comparable to urban life or better and create conditions for sustainable development. After all, rural areas in our country are 94% its area, so this is actually the space that we all live in. And that's responsibility. not only directly, the Ministry of Agriculture, but also the entire government and society. We must be aware of this and the idea for development. The protection of these interests on the part of the Ministry is ensured and this is a guarantee that it will be well implemented. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-09-sejm-51-2.u849	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! It has been 99 years since Poland regained its independence. It was reborn in a favourable political climate in Europe after the end of World War I. In this military conflict, the possessive states found themselves in opposing military and political camps. On the one hand, the central countries: Austria and Germany, on the other hand, Russia. After a long time of captivity, there was a hope of rebuilding the Polish state. A strong support for Poles was Wilson's message, which was intended to restore Poland's independence. Patriotic-national camp politicians were aware of this historic opportunity for independence. The Polish delegation spoke with a strong voice about the need to be reborn as a country with its own territory, nation and independent authorities. Spontaneously, local power centers were formed, and after Piłsudski's return from the Magdeburg Internment Center, they gave him power. The process of integrating the boundaries of individual partitions, which lasted for the next few years, began. Greater Poles, Silesians in three Silesian uprisings fought for Polish membership. In Warmia and Mazury and Powiśle there were plebiscites, whose results were to decide that these lands belonged to the reborn Republic of Poland. The population of Pomerania, together with the inhabitants of our region, also hoped to resolve the issue of territorial belonging to Poland. When it was decided to create the Pomeranian Corridor, which provided the Second Republic with access to the sea, euphoria filled the inhabitants of these lands. Under the terms of the 1919 Treaty of Versailles. Starogard and many other cities were within the borders of reborn Poland. The implementation of these provisions was supervised by General Józef Haller and his subordinate troops. A special day for the inhabitants of Starogard was on 29 January 1920 when Polish troops of the Pomeranian Front under the command of General Józef Haller arrived on the old-gardn market. This day is commemorated in our city with a solemn call of commemoration and dorms, concerts, as well as lectures. These local celebrations are attended by residents of the city, the region, as well as schoolchildren, for whom this is a unique lesson of patriotism. A year ago, it is a very good time to analyse our current geopolitical situation, to ask ourselves whether the current elites are prepared to develop the concept of European stability. Here we should return to the issue of support of Poles from 99 years ago. It wasn't anyone other than the President of the United States who was our ally. And today the United States of America is our main ally, also with the support of President Donald Trump. The speech of the President of the United States of America of July 2017 clearly shows an interest in working with a partner who could help them to ensure stability in the world, in our part of Europe. One thing is certain that the country that will present the stabilisation project to the US leaders of this region of the world can count on the support of the US administration. There are a few facts to support this thesis. Poland survived the time of communism best, the Church was the basis of opposition, it is with us that we remember the banishment of the Bolsheviks in 1920, it is in Poland that we enjoy the memory of Solidarity, the great John Paul. II. Who can boast about this in Europe? Poles can honestly boast of the lack of collaboration with Germany during World War II. That is why Poland is the last bastion of Western civilization in Europe. This is why we can count on future leadership in this part of Europe, and we owe all this to the heroes of 1918, their wisdom and predictability. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The party election system has misled our citizens' self-preservation instincts. The governing bodies of the parties ensure that the system remains unchanged and that the electoral authority is maintained, and the Constitution of the Republic of Poland practically prevents citizens from expressing their decisions in the form of a referendum. Even in the event of a collection of 500,000. signatures under the request of a referendum, the decision to carry it out is still in the hands of the Sejm. There is an insect that paralyzes the caterpillar of another species with its venom. It's devoid of movement, but it still becomes a pantry for the next generation of insect that caught it. In this way the virus of the party choices, so-called. proportional ordination, stunned Poles. They willingly support party teams in a competition called elections, not thinking that their country has fallen from bad government. In order to reverse the course towards disaster, you need to find outstanding people to rule Poland. There are no more important matters than a reasonable choice of Members. How can patriots, wise, responsible, tough, but capable of mutual respect and cooperation, with the confidence and support of the voters, be in the Sejm? How to introduce outstanding engineers, economists, lawyers, doctors, teachers, directors, officers, councillors, councillors? How can we prevent foreign business agents, eloquent undergraduates, perpetual statesmen, clever scandalists? The experience of democracy is clear in this regard. The nationwide movement to change the electoral system - one-mandate electoral districts has been proclaiming this answer for years. You have to choose people, not parties. Men of service, talent and principles. We want such an electoral ordination to be able to select candidates in every small constituency and then choose from among them a Member who will receive the most votes of support in the district. Straight and logical, cheap and fair, in a one-mandate constituency. This is a condition necessary to get Poland out of the impasse, a condition of sine qua non. This is the subject to which we will return with stubbornness, how to change the electoral system, to the Sejm, as well as to local governments, to choose people, not letters. This is a goal worth sacrificing your mind and heart. High Izbo!
